原油高一服も欧州の燃料高は続く 
インフレのカギ握るクラック・スプレッド

○ エネルギー価格が主導し消費者物価の伸びは加速。原油価格高騰一服の傍ら、燃料価格が高止
まりしたことが背景。両者の価格差であるクラック・スプレッドは足元で拡大が続いている 
○ 主因は製油所のマージンやコストの増加。中東やロシアの精製能力低下を受けた石油精製品の
需給ひっ迫・余剰精製能力の乏しさが重なり、欧州では特にクラック・スプレッド拡大が顕著 
○ エネルギーインフレの長期化懸念が高まる中、ECBによる利上げ局面が続くとの見方が出てい
る。現状景気は底堅いが、引き締め的な政策金利水準が継続し、景気を冷やすリスクには留意 
１．動きが異なる 2 つのエネルギー価格 
欧州のエネルギーインフレが加速している。8月のユーロ圏消費者物価指数は、 エネルギーの押し上
げ幅が拡大したことを背景に、2カ月連続で伸びを高めた（図表1）。エネルギーは中東情勢が緊迫化
した3月から8月までの間、 平均して前月比＋2％超のペースで上昇している。 これは、 大規模なエネル
ギーショックに見舞われた2022年の同時期に匹敵する伸び率である。 
この間、原油価格は一本調子で上昇してきたわけではない。2022年を振り返ると、ブレント原油先
物は同期間、月平均で100ドル／バレルを上回って推移した。これに対し、今次局面では一時120ドル
／バレル付近まで急騰した4月をピークに、8月までは上昇が一服していた。にもかかわらず、原油を
原料とするガソリンやディーゼルなどの石油精製品の価格は高止まりし、2022年の同時期と同程度の
水準に達している（図表2）。

### Page 2

2

エネルギー価格の指標には様々なものがあり、金融市場で注目が集まりやすいのはブレントやWTIと
いった原油価格のベンチマークだが、家計が実際に直面するのはガソリンやディーゼルなどの燃料小
売価格である。生活実感やインフレ期待への影響を考えるうえでは、むしろ後者の動向が重要になろ
う。最近では、石油精製品の価格と原油価格の差、いわゆる「クラック・スプレッド」に注目が集ま
っている。実際、ラガルドECB総裁は7月の金融政策決定会合後の記者会見で、原油価格そのものに加
えてクラック・スプレッドを含む幅広い指標を注視していると説明した。 
本稿では、足元でクラック・スプレッドが拡大している要因と、先行きの物価に与える影響や金融
政策への含意について考察する。 
２．精製能力の低下を受けて製油所のコスト・マージンが増加 
まずは消費者段階の燃料価格の構成要素を確認してみよう。Friderike Kuik et al. (2026) によ
れば、ユーロ圏の燃料価格は5つの要素、すなわち原油、精製コスト・マージン、流通コスト・マージ
ン、物品税、付加価値税（VAT）に分解できる。原油は給油所等でガソリンやディーゼルとして販売さ
れる前に、製油所で精製・加工する必要がある。この際に発生するコストや精製業者の利益が原価に
上乗せされる（精製コスト・マージン）。さらに、精製された燃料を販売拠点に運ぶ輸送費や店舗の
運営費用もここに加わる （流通コ ス ト・マージン）。そこに1リットルあたり固定額の物品税が課され、
最後に税引き前価格と物品税の合計に対して定率で付加価値税（VAT）が上乗せされる。 
これらの要素は最近の燃料価格にいかなる影響を与えたのだろうか。ディーゼル価格を例にとり、
中東情勢が緊迫化する以前の2月と、 原油価格がピークに達した4月を比較すると、 原油と精製コスト ・
マージン、 流通コスト ・ マージンを足し合わせた税引き前価格が78セント／Lから126セント／Lと大き
く上昇している一方、税額（物品税と付加価値税の合計）はほとんど変化していない（図表3）。これ
は、急激な油価高騰のショックを緩和するため、複数の国で燃料に対する物品税や付加価値税が時限
的に引き下げられたことに起因する。もっとも、こうした措置の多くは6月末までに終了している。 
注目すべきは、2月から7月にかけて、精製コスト・マージンの寄与が拡大している点である。 
背景には、精製能力の低下に伴う世界的な供
給制約がある。現在紛争の渦中にある中東、そ
してウクライナとの紛争が継続しているロシア
は、いずれも石油精製品の輸出において大きな
存在感を持つ。これらの地域・国に位置する一
部の製油所は紛争で攻撃を受けて 設備 が損傷
し、操業に支障が生じている。こうした事態を
受け、中東からの供給が大幅に抑制されている
他、ロシアでは自国の需要を賄うべく、ガソリ
ンやディーゼルなど一部石油製品を対象とした
輸出規制を2025年以降断続的に実施している。 
精製コスト・マージンはまた、求められる製
品仕様の変更や、使用する原油の種類の変化に
よっても変動する。中東での紛争激化によって

### Page 3

3

原油の調達先変更を迫られた製油所では、従来と異なる性状の原油を処理することで精製効率が低下
し、追加的なコストが生じている可能性がある。需給の引き締まりや追加コストの発生を背景に、精
製コスト・マージンがクラック・スプレッドを押し上げる状況は世界各地でみられている（図表4）。 
加えて欧州では、固有の供給減少要因も影響していよう。アジアや米国では精製能力が維持もしく
は増強されてきた一方、欧州では脱炭素化を進める過程で製油所の閉鎖が続いており、精製能力が縮
小してきた（図表5） 。 このため、 外部からの供給ショックが生じた際に域内の余剰能力で補う余地が
小さく、石油精製品需給がひっ迫しやすい構造にある。こうした要因も相まって、欧州のクラック・
スプレッドは他の国・地域と比べてとりわけ大きく上昇している。 
３．エネルギー価格の高止まりで ECB はタカ派に傾斜？ 
前節でみたように、足元の燃料価格の高止まりは、原油価格そのものよりも石油精製品の需給ひっ
迫を背景とするクラック・スプレッドの拡大によるところが大きい。紛争によって損傷した製油設備
の復旧や輸出インフラの回復には時間を要するため、石油精製品の供給制約とそれに伴うクラック・
スプレッドの拡大は、一朝一夕には解消しないとみられる。実際、IEA（2026）によれば、2026年通年
の世界の製油所処理量は前年から日量250万バレル減少する見込みであり、これは前年の処理量の約
3％に相当する。加えて、原油価格自体の動向も気がかりだ。ブレント原油先物価格は8月以降じりじ
りと上昇し、 本稿執筆時点で再び100ドル／バレルに接近している。以上を踏まえれば、 今後も燃料価
格が高止まりし、消費者段階のエネルギー価格を押し上げる状況が続く可能性が高い。 
さらに、熱波到来による電力需給のひっ迫もエネルギー価格に上昇圧力をもたらしている。川畑・
北川（2026）が指摘したように、河川水位の低下や水温上昇で冷却水が確保できずに原子力発電所の
稼働が低下すると、その不足分をガス火力発電で補う必要が生じる。この結果、天然ガス需要が増加
し、天然ガス価格は原油価格を上回るペースで上昇している。石油精製品に加えて電力・ガス価格に
も上昇圧力がかかり、エネルギーインフレの長期化リスクは一段と高まっている。

### Page 4

こうした中、 金融市場はECBによる複数回の追加利上げを見込んでいる（図表6） 。9月の理事会での
追加利上げはほぼ確実視されている他、その後も利上げが続き、政策金利（ECB預金金利） は年末にか
けて3％近くまで上昇するとの予想である。加えて一部のECB高官からも、 物価情勢次第では9月以降の
利上げを排除しない旨の発言がみられる。例えばアイルランド中央銀行のマクルーフ総裁は、物価上
振れリスクが著しく高まった場合、政策金利を引き締め的な水準（同氏によれば2.75％を超える水準）
まで引き上げる準備をすべきだと発言している。 
もっとも、今回のインフレ加速がエネルギー価格など供給要因を主因とするなか、追加利上げによ
る需要抑制が景気に及ぼす影響には注意が必要である。2026年4～6月期の銀行貸出調査 （Bank Lending 
Survey）によれば、住宅ローン向けの貸出条件を引き締めたと回答した銀行の数は、緩和したと回答
した銀行の数を上回った。これは、高インフレに対応するためECBが 急ピッチで利上げを進めていた
2023年7～9月期以来、初めてのことである。同調査は住宅ローン金利の上昇が需要を押し下げたこと
も示唆しており、実際にドイツの新規住宅ローン契約件数は前年に比べ約20％減と、こちらも2023年
夏以来の減少幅となっている（図表7）。ドイツの住宅ローンは1年超の固定金利期間付きが主流であ
り、利上げの効果が明確に表れるまでには一定の時間がかかるとみられる。そのため、2026年6月に実
施された利上げが足元の住宅ローン金利にどの程度影響を与えたかは判然としない。しかし、今後も
利上げ局面が続けば、銀行の資金調達コスト上昇を通じた一層の住宅ローン金利上昇や、それに伴う
住宅需要の減少を招く可能性は否定できない。 現状、欧州の景気は総じて底堅く推移しているものの、
エネルギーインフレが長引くにつれ、ECBには景気維持とインフレ抑制のバランス確保が求められるこ
とになるだろう。

Document 069: 25年来罕见！10年期美债收益率反超标普500盈利收益率

Source PDF: all report pdf/25年来罕见！10年期美债收益率反超标普500盈利收益率.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

10 年期美国国债收益率突破 5% ，债券相对股票的吸引⼒攀升⾄ 25 年来最⾼。以标普 500 指
数市盈率倒数衡量的股票盈利收益率，⽬前已低于 10 年期美国国债收益率，债券在收益层
⾯对股市形成明显压制。 Shiller 模型警⽰，未来⼗年标普 500 每年跑赢债券或仅约 1% 。 20
年来股优于债的投资范式正式终结。
摘要：
债券与股票之间的收益率差已逆转⾄ 25 年以来最有利于债券的⽔平，这⼀结构性转变正迫使投
资者重新审视资产配置逻辑。
10 年期美国国债收益率突破 5% ，使债券相较股票的吸引⼒达到约 25 年来最⾼⽔平。以标普 500
指数市盈率倒数衡量的股票盈利收益率，⽬前已低于 10 年期美国国债收益率，这意味着债券在
收益层⾯已对股票形成明显压制。
这⼀格局对股市构成潜在下⾏压⼒。耶鲁⼤学经济学家 Robert Shiller 的周期调整超额 CAPE 收
益率模型显⽰，未来⼗年标普 500 指数每年跑赢债券的幅度可能仅为 1% 左右。投资者对企业盈
利预期的容错空间已⼤幅收窄。

⻓债投资者遭受重创，泡沫破裂痕迹明显
⾼收益率在某种程度上是经济韧性的体现 —— 美股接近历史⾼点，美国经济表现强于预期，⻓
端利率上⾏的冲击⽬前尚未在宏观层⾯充分显现。
然⽽，率先承压的是此前押注⻓期债券的投资者。以追踪 20 年期及以上美国国债的 TLT ETF 为
参照，这类投资者已遭受⼤幅亏损。
回望过去，危机后⼗年低通胀与政府⼲预共同催⽣的债券⽜市，以及疫情期间的价格急升，事
后来看颇具资产泡沫特征。
市场规律表明，泡沫破裂往往意味着买⼊时机的到来。当前 5% 以上的 10 年期国债收益率，是数
⼗年来罕⻅的⽔平，债券的配置价值已得到实质性重估。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
2 条评论
收益率倒挂逻辑终结，⼆⼗年股优于债的范式已被打破
过去的市场正统观念认为，股票因具备增⻓潜⼒，理应享有相对于债券的估值溢价，即股票盈
利收益率⾃然低于债券收益率。
然⽽，全球⾦融危机后⻓达约⼆⼗年的时间⾥，这⼀逻辑被彻底颠覆 —— 股票盈利收益率⻓期
⾼于债券收益率，令股票成为⽆可争议的优势资产。
如今，这⼀格局再度逆转。将股票盈利收益率与 10 年期美国国债收益率相⽐较，债券的相对吸
引⼒已回到约 25 年前的⽔平。
这意味着，即便股市市盈率已有所收缩以消化更⾼的债券收益率，股市前景仍⾯临⼀定的压
⼒。
Shiller 模型发出警⽰，但历史局限性同样值得关注
Robert Shiller 的超额 CAPE 收益率指标，通过⽐较经周期调整的实际盈利收益率与 10 年期国债

收益率，来预测股票未来⼗年的超额回报。
历史数据显⽰，该指标对未来⼗年股票超额收益具有显著的预测效⼒，⽽当前读数仅暗⽰标普
500 指数在未来⼗年中每年跑赢债券约 1% 。
不过，该模型近年来的预测精度有所下滑 —— 实际股市表现远超其预期，这或许与市场持续受
到政策⼲预有关。
尽管如此，核⼼信号仍然清晰：债券的替代价值已是⼀代⼈以来最⾼。在这⼀背景下，投资者
需要以更为严苛的标准审视标普 500 的盈利增⻓预测，因为⼀旦企业利润不及预期，债市将不再
提供任何缓冲。

Document 172: 8月工业企业利润数据点评：利润分化格局持续，成本压力上行

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月工业企业利润数据点评：利润分化格局持续，成本压力上行.pdf
Report source: 万联证券
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
利润分化格局持续，成本压力上行

[Table_ReportType] 
——8 月工业企业利润数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026 年 09 月 29 日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 企业利润增速持续放缓，营收增速上行。8 月规上工业企业利
润累计同比继续下跌 1.9 个百分点至 15.7%，当月同比增速为
4.2%，前值为11.2%。营业收入增速由 6.5%上行至6.6%。从不
同类型企业来看，国企、股份制企业利润下滑提速，私企利润
持续放缓，外企利润边际修复，企业杠杆率整体小幅抬升。 
⚫ 营收利润率同比涨幅缩窄，工业品价格再度上行，工业生产增
速回温。8 月，PPI 同比由3.5%回升至 3.8%，输入性通胀压力
持续， 价格上下游传导依然受阻， 上下游企业利润或持续分化。
能源产业链价格预期仍将维持高位震荡，PPI 在油价支撑下预
期延续高位。 工业增加值同比由4.5%上行至5.2%， 工业生产边
际修复，其中高技术产业为主要拉动。营业收入利润率累计值
与上月的5.66%持平， 同比上涨0.42 个百分点，涨幅有所缩窄。 
⚫ 企业成本持续上行，费用压力 逐步缓解，应收账款回收期延长 。
每百元营业收入中的成本继续上涨 0.07 元至 85.07 元，上游资源
品价格走高给企业带来的影响持续 。企业每百元营业收入中的费
用由 8.41 元回落为 8.35 元， 减税降费等纾困政策效果逐步显现，
费用压力持续缓解。 应收账款同比上行， 产业链上下游企业博弈加
剧，带动应收账款账期拉长。 
⚫ 采矿业利润持续上行， 制造业结构分化，AI 驱动特征持续。煤炭
开采和洗选业、石油和天然气开采业增速上行，为采矿业主要
拉动。地产、基建投资尚未修复，上游黑色金属、非金属矿采
选业和加工制造业利润增速下滑。能化产业链分化持续，化纤
制造利润维持高位，化学原料增速持续回落。中游设备制造整
体利润回升。下游制造业中，计算机、通信等设备制造增速高
位上行，运输设备增速放缓。消费品制造业利润表现分化，食
品饮料加工增速放缓，医药、烟草、文娱用品等边际修复。 
⚫ “量价齐升”驱动营收上行，企业库存持续累积。营业收入在
工业生产回暖、工业品价格推动下回升。工业产成品库存持续
累积，但累库速度放缓。内需依然偏弱，资源品涨价给下游企
业带来成本压力，企业现金流和利润空间仍面临一定约束。未
来仍需关注PPI 以及上下游行业价格剪刀差变化，中下游制造
业企业成本刚性问题短期难以迎来实质性缓解。新经济利润表
现预期持续好于旧经济。海外政策未来仍存在一定波动，海外
货币政策短期收紧的趋势不变。我国政策仍将注重稳经济，四
季度财政资金有望逐步落地， “六张网”将发挥重要支撑作用。 
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策推进和落地不及预
期，消费、基建修复持续偏弱。     
[Table_Authors] 分析师: 于天旭 
执业证书编号： S0270522110001 
电话： 17717422697 
邮箱： yutx@wlzq.com.cn

事件 
2026年1-8月，全国规模以上工业企业实现利润总额5.27万亿元，累计同比较上月下
跌1.9个百分点至15.7%，8月当月同比增速由上月的11.2%回落为4.2%。

1    企业利润增速持续放缓，工业品价格再度上行 
企业利润增速持续回落， 营收增速高位上行， 营收利润率同比保持上涨。2026年8月，
全国规模以上工业企业利润累计同比由7月的17.6%继续回落至15.7%，当月同比增速
为4.2%， 前值为11.2%。规模以上企业营业收入累计同比由6.5%上行至6.6%，达到2022
年12月以来高点。营业收入利润率累计值与上月的5.66%持平，同比上涨0.42个百分
点，涨幅有所缩窄。 
工业品价格再度上行， 工业生产增速回温。8月，PPI同比由3.5%回升至3.8%， 累计同
比由1.8%上行至2%， 输入性通胀压力持续， 价格上下游传导依然受阻， 下游企业利润
相对承压，上下游企业利润或持续分化。能源产业链价格预期仍将维持高位震荡，PPI
在油价支撑下预期延续高位。 工业增加值同比由4.5%上行至5.2%， 工业生产边际修复，
其中高技术产业为主要拉动。

图表1：工业企业利润增速高位持续放缓（%） 图表2：拆分看，工业品价格上行（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

企业单位成本持续上行， 费用压力逐步缓解，但对冲有限， 应收账款回收期延长 。8月，
企业每百元资产实现的营业收入由72.2元上行至72.4元。每百元营业收入中的成本 继续
上涨0.07元至85.07元，企业成本刚性问题延续，上游资源品价格走高给企业带来的影响
持续。企业每百元营业收入中的费用由8.41元回落为8.35元，减税降费等纾困政策效果
逐步显现，费用压力持续缓解。 产成品周转天数持平于上期的21.3天。 规模以上工业企业
应收账款同比由7月的8.5%上行至9%，应收账款平均回收期环比由71.9天上行至72.2天，
产业链上下游企业博弈加剧，带动应收账款账期拉长。

国企、 股份制企业利润下滑提速，私企利润持续放缓， 外企利润边际修复，企业杠杆
率整体小幅抬升。2026年8月，分不同企业类型来看，国有企业利润累计同比增速由
上月的16.3%进一步下行至10.3%，下滑速度加快。股份制企业利润累计同比由23.6%
回落至20.4%，跌幅边际加大。私营企业由10.9%回落至10.4%，下滑速度放缓。外资
企业利润累计同比增速由1.2%回升至2.3%， 利润依然相对承压。 仅私企资产负债率与
上月持平，国企、外企和股份制企业资产负债率均延续小幅抬升。 
图表5：国企、股份制企业利润回落幅度较大（%） 图表6：国企、股份制企业、外企资产负债率上行(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

2    采矿业为主要支撑，制造业利润结构持续分化 
采矿业利润增速继续上行，制造业利润增速回落， 电热气生产和供应业跌幅加深。三
大工业行业来看， 采矿业利润累计同比增速由34.9%继续上行至35.1%，为利润主要支
撑项。从细分行业来看，煤炭开采和洗选业利润累计同比增速上行 1.2个百分点至
51.6%，上行速度放缓，随天气温度下降，电力需求边际回落。煤炭开采行业内部供
需格局持续改善，前期成本持续压降给利润带来支撑。国际油价上行推动下， 石油和
天然气开采业增速反弹上行。有色金属矿采选业增速 高位 持续回落， 高库存带来一定
压制；黑色金属矿采选业增速由正转负，非金属矿采选业增速跌幅加深， 回落幅度相
对最大， 地产、 建筑开工偏弱， 给上游黑色金属、 非金属矿采选业带来一定拖累。 制
造业利润总额累计同比由18.8%进一步回落至17.4%， 产成品周转天数与上月持平。电

制造业新旧动能转换特征持续，AI依然为主要驱动，消费品制造业利润表现分化。从
制造业细分行业来看，黑色金属冶炼及压延加工业跌幅加深， 非金属矿物制品跌幅边
际收敛， 地产投资偏弱的影响持续。 有色金属冶炼及压延加工业利润同比回落，整体
仍处高位。8月原油价格环比再度走高，美伊局势仍有较大不确定性，油价预期维持
高位震荡， 从下游相关产业链来看， 化学纤维制造业高位持平，纺织服装、 服饰业跌
幅持续收窄；化学原料及化学制品制造业增速持续回落，地产链条相关品种需求偏弱，
农产品产业链等品种前期扩产过多， 景气度持续回落。 中游设备制造业整体利润回升，
电气机械及器材制造业利润跌幅收窄，通用设备制造业增速回升， 仅专用设备制造业
增速由正转负，外需仍具韧性，出口需求支撑持续。下游制造业利润增速表现分化，
计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增速由105%进一步上行至109.9%，AI高速发展，
价格与需求上行共同推动行业利润走高； 铁路、 船舶、 航空航天和其他运输设备制造
业增速由12.2%回落至10.9%， 汽车制造业由-20.4%回升至-16%。消费品制造业表现分
化，酒、饮料和精制茶制造业利润跌幅相对最大，高基数带来一定影响，食品制造、
农副食品加工业增速进一步放缓；家具制造业增速反弹上行， 但增速仍处低位；医药、
烟草、文娱用品制造业利润增速边际修复，内需偏弱依然带来一定拖累。

3    企业库存持续累积 
“量价齐升”驱动营收上行，企业库存持续累积。营业收入在工业生产回暖、 工业品
价格推动下回升。工业产成品存货累计同比由10.8%继续上行至11.0%， 库存持续累积，
但累库速度放缓。内需依然偏弱， 上游资源品涨价给下游企业带来成本压力， 企业现
金流和利润空间仍面临一定约束。 未来仍需关注PPI以及上下游行业价格剪刀差变化，
中下游制造业企业成本刚性问题短期难以迎来实质性缓解。 新经济利润表现预期持
续好于旧经济。海外政策不确定性指数在8月边际回落，但在地缘政治风险持续，油
价高位的影响下， 未来仍存在一定波动， 海外货币政策短期收紧的趋势不变。 我国政
策仍将注重稳经济，四季度财政资金有望逐步落地，“六张网”将发挥重要的支撑作
用。

图表11：工业企业库存持续上行（%） 图表12：美国、日本政策不确定性指数阶段性回落

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

4    风险提示 
地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策推进和落地不及预期，消费、基建修复持续偏弱。

强于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％以上； 
同步大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％至-10％之间； 
弱于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘跌幅10％以上。

[Table_StockRankInfo] 公司投资评级 
买入：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅15％以上； 
增持：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅5％至15％； 
观望：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅-5％至5％； 
卖出：未来6个月内公司相对大盘跌幅5％以上。

[Table_RiskInfo] 风险提示 
我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。 我们采用的是相对评级体系， 表示投资的
相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考
虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。

出具本报告。 本报告清晰准确地反映了本人的研究观点。 本人不曾因， 不因， 也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意
见或观点而直接或间接收到任何形式的补偿。

SK하이닉스  
(000660) 
 소음보다 방향성에

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 3,200,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.09.23) 
1,862,000 
반도체업종

◼ 최근 시장은 2가지 Risk를 제기. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 
◼ 현 주가는 보다 강인해지는 Fundamental의 가치를 미반영한 수준. 
◼ 비중 확대 전략 유효. 목표주가와 매수의견 유지.

투자전략: 업황을 대변하지 못하고 있는 주가 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 현 주가는 업황의 강세를 충분히 반영하지 못하
고 있다고 생각. 1) 메모리반도체 사이클의 추가 확장 (구매 경쟁 심화에 따른 
단기 가격 상승 폭 확대, 기대치를 상회할 4Q26 DRAM 판매량), 2) 3Q26 실
적발표에서 보다 구체화될 주주환원 정책의 방향성에 보다 집중할 필요. 
시장에서 제기하는 Risk가 있다면, 2가지. 1) 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈, 
2) Solidigm IPO와 해외 공장 투자에 따른 경계심리 확대가 그 대상. 당사는 
해당 Risk가 Fundamental을 저해할 것으로 보고 있지 않으며, 단기 노이즈보
다는 사이클 확장의 선순환에 보다 주목할 필요가 있다고 생각. 
1. HBM4 품질 이슈, 과도한 우려는 기우 
3Q26부터 HBM4 출하가 본격화될 것이라는 기존 입장을 유지. HBM4 비중
은 3Q26 전체 판매량의 40%, 2027년 연간 50% 중반까지 상승할 것. 
일각에서는 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈로 사업 성과가 기대치를 하회할 
수 있다는 우려를 표출. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 후공정 단에서 문제가 일
부 발생했을 수 있으나, 해결 불가능한 Risk라 보기 어렵고, 불량에 따른 배
상금을 논할 단계도 아니라는 판단. 
2. 해외 공장 투자, 실보다 득이 크다 
언론보도에 따르면, SK하이닉스는 해외 공장 투자와 Solidigm IPO를 추진 중. 
해당 결정이 현실화될 경우, 시장은 단기적으로 이를 부정적으로 받아들일 
가능성이 존재. 다만, 결과적으로, 실보다 득이 큰 선택이 될 것이라 생각. 
- 해외 투자가 현실화되더라도, 실질 생산 기여 시점은 2028년 이후일 가능
성이 높음. 미국의 건설 경기와 공장 구축의 세부 단계 등을 감안할 필요. 
- 해외 투자 결정이 관세 Risk를 축소시킬 수 있는 변화라는 점도 감안할 필
요. “불확실성 해소”는 Upcycle의 중반부에서 반도체 주식에 대한 좋은 투자
포인트가 되어왔고, 이번에도 다르지 않을 것이라는 판단.

Editorial 
A Fed credibility-induced bounce  
Strategy summary:  The Fed’s unanimous decision to hike 
rates this week for the first time since 2023 , and pencil in 
another hike by December, confirms our expectations – flagged 
in the last two weeklies - of a central bank willing to establish its 
independence against political pressure. The Fed’s moderately 
hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. 
The BoJ also hiked rates this week. It is the first time on record 
all three major central banks – Fed, ECB and BoJ – have hiked 
in the same month , as they respond primarily to a resurgence 
in energy-driven inflation amid tight job markets.  
We expect the Fed to hike once more by year-end to 4.0-4.25% 
and follow up with another hike in H1 2027 to 4.25 -4.5%. With 
the revised estimates, we revise up our US government bond 
yield forecasts. Nevertheless, with Fed policy uncertainty 
easing, we see an opportunity to use the equity market dip to 
add exposure amid solid corporate earnings.  
Fed moves to restore credibility:  The Fed’s policy pivot this 
week followed Chair Warsh’s hawkish Jackson Hole speech 
and recent economic data prints, namely: a pick -up in job 
creation and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in 
oil prices due to the re -escalation of the Middle E ast conflict. 
Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike rates this week, we 
expect two more hikes by  June 2027 on the back of  robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated. We expect this pivot to help the Fed 
rebuild credibility as an inflation fighter.  
Policy outlook less aggressive than market pricing : The 
updated Fed policy rate estimates are lower compared with 
money market pricing of three more rate hikes by June 2027 
since we expect disinflation to take hold by Q2 2027 as the 
impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. We believe policy rates can 
return to the 4.0–4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility 
of cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures ease.  
Upgrading near-term US rates and USD estimates : Based 
on these revised estimates, we also upgrade our 3-month target 
for the US 10 -year government bond yield to 5.0 -5.25%. We 
also revise up our 12 -month target for the US 10 -year bond 
yield to 4.75-5.0% (see page 4).  
Buying the equity dip as focus turns to fundamentals: Fed 
hikes typically do not hurt equities over the medium-term when 
accompanied by strong earnings growth. We estimate a 25bps 
rise in Fed rates (the discount rate) would impact the S&P500 
index and global equities by 3 -4%. However, we expect the 
headwind from higher rates to be offset by strong earnings 
growth, driving positive performance for global equities.  
Furthermore, a hawkish Fed is largely priced in and we expect 
the Fed to deliver fewer rate hikes than currently priced by 
markets. Meanwhile, we believe concerns over AI industry 
leaders slowing the pace of development are overdone. Hence, 
we maintain our preference for a diversified group of large cap 
technology and semiconductor industry leaders (see page 5-6). 
BoJ hikes; modestly bullish JPY : The BoJ hiked rates by 
25bps to a three-decade high of 1.25%, as expected, but the 7-
2 split vote led to renewed JPY weakness on concerns it may 
slow the pace of further hikes. We remain constructive on JPY 
amid narrowing rate differential vs peers as the BoJ potentially 
hikes rates by 25bps once per quarter until Q2 2027 to curb 
rising domestic wage-driven inflation pressure . Any sign of BoJ 
slowing the pace of hikes could lead to renewed JPY weakness, 
fuelling inflationary pressures (see page 7).  
BoE holds; rangebound GBP: The BoE held rates this week, 
bucking the hiking trend among peers, as weakening UK job 
and housing markets offset rebound in energy-driven inflation. 
We expect BoE to deliver fewer rate hikes than the 100bps of 
increase currently priced by markets  as the job market slows 
further. This is likely to keep GBP rangebound (see page 7).  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions 
  Where do you see US government bond  yields following 
the Fed’s 16 September decision to hike interest rates? 
Our view: The US 10-year government bond yield is likely to 
retest the 5.00-5.25% range in the near term. We favour 3-7-year 
maturity bond s in t he US, preferring credit over government 
bonds. We are looking for an opportunity to extend duration 
approaching Q2 2027. 
Rationale: Over the next few months, we expect 2- and 30-year US 
government bond yields to remain rangebound, but the 10-year yield 
is expected to rebound up to the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons for 
this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially rise further 
due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, with Saudi 
Arabia’s East-West oil pipeline shut, the Houthis now firmly in control 
of the Strait of Bab-al-Mandab, the Strait of Hormuz still closed and 
China returning as a buyer of crude oil in the market ; 2) despite 
notable AI leaders expressing the need to slow AI development, AI 
capex plans are showing no signs of slowing down, which continues 
to drive above-trend growth in the economy. This, in turn, also means 
AI-related heavy bond issuance  is expected to continue. These 
issuers typically issue long-dated tenors, and this competition for 
capital will also exert upward pressure on the US 10-year yields. 
We note that the US 10-year bond yield has room to move lower, 
driven by short-covering activities in the very near term as net-short 
positions had moved to extreme levels before th is week’s  Fed 
meeting. However, we think such downshifts would be short-lived 
and the central tendency for the 10-year yield would be to move back 
up to the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Meanwhile, we see US 30-year yields continuing to reflect the dire 
fiscal outlook for the US government. We expect US 2-year yields 
to continue trading in a range that reflects expectations of further 
federal funds rate hikes. For the record, we only expect two more 
rate hikes from the Fed into the middle of 2027 , while money 
markets are pricing in three more for the same period. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate (either 
through a resolution of the M iddle East conflict or a re-routing of oil 
supply chains)  and the inflationary effects from tariffs to fade. 
Inflation by then is likely be close to – if not at – the Fed’s target of 
2%. At this point, we see room for the Fed to contemplate rate 
cuts. US 2-year yields (the tenor that is most sensitive to Fed policy 
changes) will likely start to price th is in. With oil prices and inflation 
cooling, 10-year yields would also have room to come down. 
US 30-year yields, on the other hand, are expected to  remain 
elevated as the US fiscal deficit remains substantial next year and 
could become much worse than its already dire state today. As a 
result, the ‘term premium ’ may keep the ultra -long end elevated. 
Short of some unorthodox policy to artificially cap them lower, 30 -
year yields could continue to drift higher into H2 2027. 
We continue to favour the 3-7-year segment of the yield curve and 
prefer credit over government bond s. We see an opportunity for 
us to extend duration closer to Q2 2027. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

for US government bond yields

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Will the step -up in the  Fed’s risk-free rate trigger a re -
pricing of equities? 
Our view: We expect strong corporate earnings growth to offset 
the headwind of higher rates , driving positive performance for 
global equities. We remain Overweight global equities. 
Rationale: The Fed’s latest 25bps rate hike had been anticipated 
by the bond market, partly reflected in the bond yield  rise we have 
seen so far in 2026. Using the US 10-year government bond yield as 
a proxy for the risk -free rate, the yield has risen by 80-85bps this 
year. We estimate that each 25bps rise in the discount rate presents 
a valuation drag on global equities. On this basis, the rise in yields 
represents a potential 10-14% headwind for equities this year. 
However, the Fed’s hike reflects a solid expansion of US economic 
activity, resilient domestic spending and robust capital investment. 
This is consistent with the positive earnings revisions we have seen 
this year, with  2026 earnings growth projections now at 34% (and 
16% for 2027). Global equities are up 14% year-to-date (YTD), but 
even with a 14% valuation headwind from higher yields, we believe 
they can rise further, given strong earnings growth in 2026 and 2027. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is the outlook for the US 10-year government bond 
yield? What is your tactical view on the DXY? 
Our view: We raise our three-month US 10 -year government 
bond yield forecast to the 5.00-5.25% range. Meanwhile, we see 
a near-term upward bias in the DXY after its recent downtrend, 
supported by recent Fed tightening and higher  short-term US 
bond yields.  
Rationale: The Fed 's decision  to raise the target range for the 
federal funds rate by 25bps to 3.75-4.00% validates the upside policy 
risks we flagged recently, following Fed Chair Warsh's Jackson Hole 
speech and a run of firmer US economic data. We expect growth 
to remain robust, the labour market  to remain tight and inflation to 
stay elevated, supporting one more hike before year-end 2026 and 
potentially another in H1 2027  – fewer than the three additional 
hikes currently priced in by money markets . For the US 10-year 
government bond yield, further policy tightening and lingering US 
fiscal concerns should keep yields elevated, but the Fed’s visible 
response to inflation should limit additional upward pressure, leaving 
the 10-year yield holding around the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Crucially, Warsh provided no forward guidance on further rate 
hikes. Markets had already priced in substantial additional rate hikes 
ahead of the Fed meeting, reducing the scope for further USD upside 
purely from rate repricing. Meanwhile, we expect further policy 
tightening from the ECB and the BoJ, while the RBA also remains 
biased towards further tightening. This should gradually narrow the 
US rate advantage and limit the extent of a DXY rebound. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

Global equities’ earnings growth estimates have

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in technology as AI lab 
CEOs call for a slowdown in frontier AI model development? 
Our view: We believe concerns over “pacing the frontier” are 
overdone. We maintain our preference to remain invested in 
and diversified across Big Tech and semiconductor leaders. 
Rationale: We believe it is prudent for investors to avoid 
overreacting to the recent calls for a slowdown in frontier AI model 
development, as they focus on responsible AI development, rather 
than halting AI model development or reducing spending. The global 
AI leadership  race, reinforced by the US and China’s  strategic 
ambitions, also makes a coordinated slowdown unlikely. 
While the near-term narrative may shift from AI training beneficiaries 
towards inference plays, hyperscalers continue to signal strong AI 
infrastructure commitments and improving monetisation, supporting 
our AI capex outlook. Rising AI adoption and agentic use cases 
should sustain inference demand, even if training activity moderates, 
benefiting custom AI chips, NAND flash memory and hyperscalers.  
We believe the decline in valuations  this year more than 
compensates for any downside risk to AI capex. Our Opportunistic 
idea on Global Semiconductors further reflects our conviction, with 
attractive valuations supporting selective additions during volatility. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  With ASEAN and UK equities outperforming AI-focused 
markets in H2 2026, is it worth adding to these markets? 
Our view: ASEAN and UK equities offer portfolio diversification, 
but we are Underweight both markets in favour of US and Asia 
ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, given their stronger earnings potential. 
Rationale: The ASEAN and UK markets have outperformed the 
MSCI ACWI over the past three months, but both have 
underperformed on a YTD basis. Financials is the largest sector in 
the ASEAN (53%) and the UK (28%) and provides steady earnings. 
However, both markets have minimal exposure to the technology 
sector (just 3% and 1% , respectively), ultimately dragging down 
growth. Hence, while defensive markets offer value by increasing 
diversification and reducing volatility in a portfolio, we maintain our 
Underweight allocation for ASEAN and the UK, as we see much 
stronger earnings potential elsewhere in the US and AxJ markets.  
We remain Overweight US and AxJ, supported by robust earnings 
growth, as  AI still has the potential to become a major economic 
growth driver . Our 12-month S&P500 Index forecast of 8,400  
implies low-teens upside, supported by a strong earnings backdrop 
of 25-30% earnings-per-share growth for 2026 and 15 -20% for 
2027. AxJ also boasts the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, fuelled by the AI semiconductor super-cycle. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is it still appropriate to have a positive stance on US 
high-yield (HY) bonds? 
Our view: We remain opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. 
Lower sensitivity to interest rate volatility, a below-trend default 
rate and a maturity wall pushed out to 2028-29 drive this view. 
Rationale: Recent interest rate volatility has highlighted a key 
structural advantage of US HY bonds: their higher coupon rates  
and shorter tenors relative to Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds and US investment-grade (IG) corporate bonds translate into 
a much lower sensitivity to interest rates, cushioning bond prices 
during yield backup episodes, where bond yields rise sharply. 
YTD, US HY has delivered 1.8% in total returns, outperforming DM 
government bonds and US IG corporate bonds by over 3% each on 
a relative basis. This outperformance was particularly evident during 
the recent back-up in bond yields, as inflation concerns re-emerged 
after renewed Middle East tensions – an environment where HY's 
lower rate sensitivity provided a relative cushion  even as broader 
fixed income markets came under pressure in absolute terms. On 
the fundamentals side, US HY corporate credit quality remains 
resilient. Default rates continue to track below long-run trend levels, 
and the maturity wall has been pushed out to 2028 -29, reducing 
near-term refinancing risk. Against a non -recessionary macro 
backdrop, we expect these credit fundamentals to hold, supporting 
the case for continued opportunistic exposure to the asset class.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Do the latest BoJ and BoE meetings alter your view on the 
central banks’ rate paths and their corresponding currencies? 
Our view: We remain constructive on the JPY and expect two 
BoJ rate hikes this year and potentially one hike per quarter in 
H1 2027 as policy normalisation continues. Meanwhile, the BoE 
held rates at 3.75%. We expect GBP/USD to stay rangebound 
within 1.32-1.36 over the next 1-3 months, with downside risk 
towards the lower end of our medium-term 1.30-1.36 range. 
Rationale: Persistent inflation, higher energy prices and earlier JPY 
weakness support further BoJ policy normalisation . Continued 
hiking should progressively narrow the US -Japan rate gap, reduce 
the attractiveness of JPY -funded carry trades and support  the JPY 
over the medium term towards the 153-155 range. However, with a 
September hike largely priced  in, future tightening ’s pace  will be 
more important for the JPY. Meanwhile, the BoE was split 6-3 in its 
decision to hold rates at 3.75% , with three members preferring a 
25bps hike, signalling elevated inflation risks. However, a weaker 
labour market is likely to limit the pace of BoE tightening. The US-
UK rate divergence is expected to favour the USD over the next 1-3 
months. Further BoE tightening (if inflation remains elevated ) could 
narrow the rate gap and provide support to the GBP later in the cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

strong performance in the near term

Document 201: AI, Ageing and the Budget_ Five Takeaways from the 2026 IGR (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI, Ageing and the Budget_ Five Takeaways from the 2026 IGR (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence
AI, Ageing and the Budget: Five Takeaways from the 2026 IGR
By Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist, Westpac
18:21 September 21 2026 Share
The 2026 Intergenerational Report is the 7th in a series of such reports, dating back to
2002. The IGR sets out a budget scenario highlighting potential budget challenges over
coming decades.

### Page 2

The familiar theme is that Australia’s ageing population will put upward pressure on expenditures and
downward pressure on revenues, creating a budget challenge.
In the 2026 edition, several forces are identified as continuing to shape Australia’s economy over the
coming decades: the artificial intelligence revolution; geopolitical fragmentation; energy transition;
ageing and the care economy; and Australia’s industrial transformation.
Below are the five key themes that stood out in this year's IGR: AI to be a game-changer for

productivity; Australians will live longer; NDIS reforms are crucial to budget sustainability, but knock on
to state budgets; Bracket creep has its limits, and future spending will need to be funded elsewhere;
and Higher costs of capital here to stay.
1. AI to be a game-changer for productivity
The IGR identifies AI as having the potential to be a game-changer for productivity growth. We agree
and have written extensively about the data centre and renewable energy investment surge. This

investment will boost value added and jobs in the near term. However, the real payoff will come when
the technology is widely adopted and businesses redesign processes to get the most out of AI.
The ultimate gains are difficult to estimate, in part because of the speed of development in the
technology. Another reason is that technological change at this scale does not simply augment or
automate existing roles but creates new roles, services and goods. The Productivity Commission

estimates that AI could boost multifactor productivity in Australia by at least 2.3 per cent over the next
decade, but this estimate was published in December, before the step-change in model capability we
saw early this year.
The IGR shows that productivity matters for the budget: productivity growth 0.4ppt higher than the
assumed 1.2% per year would reduce gross debt by 25ppts of GDP in 2065–66. Of course, this
assumes that fiscal policy does not respond to the faster trend growth that implies.

### Page 3

2. Australians will live longer
The IGR projects that life expectancy will continue to increase to 86.1 for males and 89.5 for females
by 2065, around five years longer than today. This presents both opportunities and challenges.
Older Australians are remaining in the workforce for longer, boosting labour supply and economic
activity. At the same time, rising demand for health, aged care and pension spending will place
increasing pressure on government budgets.

This is one area where AI, along with governance changes, could deliver outsized benefits. While an
ageing population will increase demand for government services, productivity-enhancing technologies
can improve service delivery and support prevention.
We know that the non-market sector (including health care, education and public administration) is the
largest it has ever been, with the expansion mainly driven by health services. Notwithstanding

measurement issues, we know that productivity levels in this sector has declined recently. Greater use
of evidence-based practice, preventive care, and AI-enabled technologies in health and aged care
could help improve productivity across this rapidly expanding part of the economy.

### Page 4

Demographic change will increase reliance on a smaller share of the population to fund the health,
aged care and other services required by a growing number of older Australians. We know that the
average fiscal contribution of an individual becomes negative later in life as government spending on
health and aged care rises. By contrast, people in their prime working years make the largest net
contribution to the budget through higher workforce participation and tax payments. 
This reinforces the importance of lifting productivity growth, both to support living standards and to help
meet rising spending pressures without placing an ever-greater burden on the working-age population.

3. NDIS reforms are crucial to budget sustainability, but knock on to state budgets 
The IGR highlights just how significant the NDIS reforms are for the fiscal outlook. Without the reforms,
payments would be around 1 percentage point of GDP higher over the medium term. The savings are
substantial and reinforce the importance of full implementation, a point we have previously made
(2026–27 Australian Federal Budget | Westpac IQ ). 
The report focuses on the Commonwealth budget, but the implications for state budgets could also be
significant. To the extent that some costs are shifted to states, a complete assessment of the reforms
requires a consolidated national fiscal perspective.

### Page 5

4. Bracket creep has its limits, and future spending will need to be funded elsewhere
Personal income tax receipts are projected to rise from 12.3% of GDP in 2025-26 to 14.1% of GDP by
2065-66, even with the Government capping tax as a share of the economy.
This highlights a structural challenge. As Australia ages, an increasing share of government revenue
will come from a relatively smaller working-age population, while the share of indirect taxes continues

to decline.
The IGR argues that relying ever more heavily on labour income taxation is unlikely to be a sustainable
long-run solution. Future governments may therefore need to look elsewhere for revenue growth. This
could include reforms to indirect taxes, particularly as fuel excise revenues come under pressure from
the transition to electric vehicles.
The report reinforces the need to modernise the tax base as activity shifts towards digital services and

intangible consumption. The revenue challenge is material, with the erosion of indirect tax bases
potentially amounting to close to 1ppt of GDP over coming decades, on top of the effective loss of
tobacco excise in recent years.
The terms of trade are assumed to remain broadly unchanged from their 2027–28 level, underpinning

### Page 6

company tax receipts. This bakes in the sharp near-term decline in iron ore prices that has been
assumed in every budget for more than a decade without eventuating. Despite this, the longer-run
trajectory is increasing uncertain as the world transitions towards net zero.
5. Higher costs of capital here to stay
We have long argued that the era of ultra-low interest rates is unlikely to return. The 2026 IGR
reinforces this view. Despite an improved fiscal position and lower projected debt than in the 2023 IGR,

interest payments are projected to be higher because government borrowing costs are assumed to
remain elevated relative to the post-GFC period.
Total interest payments are projected to rise from 0.9% of GDP in 2025–26 to 1.6% of GDP in 2032–33
before easing to 1.2% of GDP by 2065–66. Importantly, the primary cash balance (excluding interest
payments) is projected to remain in surplus until the mid-2050s. In other words, the budget is projected
to be in deficit largely because of the growing cost of servicing debt, rather than an underlying

imbalance between government revenues and expenditures.
The IGR’s sensitivity analysis highlights how important borrowing costs are for the fiscal outlook. If
bond yields average 5.4 per cent, broadly in line with current levels, the underlying cash deficit would
be around 0.5 percentage points of GDP larger and gross debt would be 6.6 percentage points of GDP
higher by 2065-66.

### Page 7

Document 225: August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-09-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-09-30).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
August CPI – Came in on our expectations
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
15:27 September 30 2026 Share
August Monthly CPI: Headline 0.4%mth/4.0%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. Market
services ex volatile –0.4%mth/3.3%yr.

The CPI rose 0.4% in August, spot on Westpac’s expectation but a bit softer than market
expectations of 0.5%. Due to base effects the annual pace picked up to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline result were auto fuels (14.8%); games, toys & hobbies (10.2%) with
rents providing a solid base (0.3%) while some offset was provided by both domestic (–4.9%) and
international holiday travel & accommodation (–1.5%).
Trimmed mean (TM) inflation printed 0.2% again bang on Westpac’s expectation while the market
was slightly higher at 0.3%. The annual pace was flat at 3.6%yr, the third month in a row at this pace,
while the six-month annualise pace was steady at 3.9%yr.

Key factors driving the TM print were: rents (0.3%) and new dwelling purchases (0.2%). The TM
distribution ranged from –0.38% to 0.82%.
Today’s inflation update revealed price discounting for some durable goods as retail demand softens.
We would expect to see a bounce from these categories in the following month. There was also
offsetting strength elsewhere in durable good so at least for now, the momentum continues to
suggest some near term upside risk to our current inflation estimates.

Nevertheless, as the TM came in on expectations and based on a first quick pass at updating the
quarterly profile, we see little pressure to revise our current Q3 estimates of 1.3%qtr for the CPI and
0.9%qtr for the TM.
The RBA remains alert to near term inflationary risks. We are expecting the momentum in TM
inflation to remain well above the top of the target band through to year-end underpinning our
expectations for the RBA to raise rates again in November.

### Page 3

August CPI in more detail
Following the bumper read in July, the monthly CPI moderated in August rising 0.4%mth. This was in
line with our expectations and slightly softer than the market consensus. This took the year-ended pace
to 4.0%yr. On a seasonally adjusted basis the CPI rose 0.7%mth highlighting the impact of seasonal
sales in August.

They key drivers behind the monthly lift were automotive fuel, up 14.8%mth, contributing 0.5ppts to the
headline figure. The surge resulted from the ending of the fuel excise discount on August 3 and rising
global energy prices. With crude oil prices still elevated due to the conflict in the middle east, and
growing shortages of refined products due to both the middle east conflict and Ukrainian assaults on
Russian refining capacity, auto fuel is expected to remain an inflationary impulse at least until the end
of 2026.

The monthly TM also softened, rising 0.2%mth compared to the 0.5%mth read from July. The result
was again in line with our expectations, and very close at two decimal places (0.24%mth vs
0.23%mth), but softer than the market's view. The year-ended pace held at 3.6%yr for the third
consecutive month, although it continued to ease marginally at the second decimal place. In contrast,
the six-month annualised pace has continued to trend higher since April and is currently running at
3.9%yr.

### Page 4

There is a possibility that as the ABS continues to grapple with seasonal adjustment issues we will see
revisions to the TM estimates. Last July also saw a strong 0.48%mth read which eased to 0.29%mth in
August. This is not too dissimilar from the 0.48%mth and 0.24%mth reads for 2026.

We are expecting core inflation to start to moderate as we get to year end but our current estimate for
the month of December still has the TM running at a 3.3% in the year while the six-month annualised
pace is still a chunky 3.1%. Applying a trim on the seasonally adjusted 3m/3m expenditure classes
yields a TM of 0.9%qtr currently. This is in line with our current Q3 TM estimate.

Other measures of underlying inflation told a similar story, easing on a monthly basis but holding steady
on a year-ended basis. Market services ex volatiles fell –0.4%mth to be 3.3%yr higher. Excluding both
volatiles & holidays saw a 0.2%mth/4.1%yr lift.

Most market services components eased in the month (including on a seasonally adjusted basis) but
annual growth across this group as a whole remained above the RBA's 2-3% target band. The near-
term momentum (three-month annualised) is also quite strong across several components of services.
Of note include deposit and loan facilities (11.2%mth), sports participation (10.9%), clothing repair
(7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other household services (5.2%). We are closely watching market
services as this is where inflationary risks, outside of energy, appear to be most persistent.

Due to its sizable weight in the CPI, and the fact price changes here tend to be less volatile than many
other categories, so they almost always appear in the TM estimates, housing prices remain a key
focus. Rents rose 0.3%mth in August, in line with our expectations, to be up 3.6%yr. Since April, the
seasonally adjusted rents series has been running lower than the non-seasonally adjusted series. We
are expecting rents to hold around this pace till year end before moderating modestly as we move
through 2027.

New dwellings inflation was 0.2%mth in August (softer than our expectation for a 0.4%mth rise). Since
peaking in May, monthly price growth has slowed in each successive month, potentially reflecting
increasing difficulty for developers in passing through higher costs amid softer conditions in established
housing markets and weaker demand in the face of higher interest rates. The annual pace looks to
have topped out. It was at 5.8%yr in June easing to 5.4%yr currently with the three-month annualised
pace at 3.7%. We expect dwelling price inflation to continue easing through to the end of 2027 as
falling established house prices limit the ability of builders to pass on rising construction costs. There is
a medium-term risk that as conditions improve builders may raise prices more aggressively to claw
back margins.

### Page 5

Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated in August. The share of the basket running above 3%yr
eased from 45% to 42%, with the share below 2%yr increasing from 38% to 41%.

### Page 6

Key surprises in the month:
Upside surprises

Downside surprises
Insurance surprised to the upside (0.7%mth vs 0.3%mth) after a run of weaker-than-usual reads over
the past two months.
Motor vehicle repair costs rose a solid 0.8% against our 0.3%mth expectations, with spare parts and
accessories also up 0.3%mth vs –0.4%mth.
Footwear rose 1.7%mth against our expectations for a -2.9%mth decline, the gains were broad-
based across women, men & children.
Household goods surprised to the upside. Household appliances, utensils and tools rose 1.9%mth
against our flat expectations, meanwhile household textiles fell –1.0%mth against our expectations
for a –5.0%mth fall. Furniture and furnishings also fell by less than expected –0.6%mth vs –1.3%mth.
Recreation and culture surprised to the upside (–1.0%mth vs –1.5%mth), owing to a 1.4%mth lift in
other recreation, sport & culture against our expectations for a flat read on the back of an outsized
gain in games, toys & hobbies (10.2%mth vs 2.5%mth). Holiday travel was marginally less weak than
expected (–3.3% vs –3.5%mth).

### Page 7

Food was the key downside miss falling –0.1%mth against our expectations for a 0.2%mth lift. Meat
& seafood (–0.9%mth vs 0.5%mth) and fruit & vegetables (–1.2%mth vs 0.1%mth) were the key
areas of weakness.
Audio, visual and computing equipment and services came in weaker-than-expected (0.7%mth vs
1.2%mth), owing to a softer 0.6%mth lift in computing services (3.0%mth expected). This was partly
balanced by a stronger than expected lift in computing equipment (0.9%mth vs –0.5%mth). The latter

is where we are most likely to see the impact of the AI boom in the CPI, due to computer chip
inflation, other than construction cost inflation for new dwellings.
Motor vehicles came in lower at –0.4%mth vs –0.1%mth which could be the start of price pressures
associated with the rapid expansion of the share of Chinese cars in the Australian market.
Automotive fuel was also slightly softer at 14.8%mth vs 16.7%mth.
Garments and accessories were both softer. Garments fell –1.9%mth against our expectations for a

0.2%mth rise, meanwhile accessories came in at 0.1%mth vs 0.5%mth expected.

### Page 8

What does this mean for the RBA?
Yesterday, post the RBA rate hike and monetary policy decision statement, Westpac changed its view
from a November follow up rate hike being a risk to being the base case. The post-meeting statement
was notable for its emphasis on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices are now
much higher than had been assumed in the August forecasts”. In addition, RBA’s liaison suggests firms
are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-through was characterised as partial, and additional

to domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We read this as implying that the RBA
is concerned about upside risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.

### Page 9

Related articles
Our current forecast of 0.9%qtr for the September quarter TM stands post today’s release and
represents a step up from 0.8% in the June quarter holding the annual pace at 3.6%yr. Our current
forecast for the Q4 TM is 0.7%qtr/3.3%yr but given the move in energy prices, and anecdotes of firms
now passing on those rising costs, we too see upside risks to this estimate.

As such, we hold to the view that the RBA will follow up with another rate hike in November. For more
information on the RBA decision please see Luci Ellis’s note “RBA hikes in September, November
follow-up now expected".

利率的不确定性，叠加国庆七天⻓假期间海外变数的避险情绪，市场交投清淡。

线⾛⾼。商品⽅⾯，国内商品期货多数下跌，贵⾦属期货跌幅居前。
A 股：截⾄收盘，沪指涨 0.18% ，深成指涨 0.34% ，创业板指涨 0.09% 。
盘⾯上，个股涨多跌少，沪深京三市约 3500 股飘红，量能⼤幅萎缩，今⽇全市场成交 1.42 万
亿，创逾 14 个⽉低位。沪深两市成交额 1.41 万亿，刷新年内新低，较上⼀个交易⽇缩量近 3000
亿。板块⽅⾯，地产、⽂化传媒板块领涨，万科 A 、新华⽂轩等多股涨停；电路板、 Kimi 、虚拟
⼈概念亮眼；⽯油化⼯、海运板块跌幅居前。

逻辑链⼀：国常会 " 研究出台稳定房地产市场政策措施 " 。
9 ⽉ 28 ⽇召开的国务院常务会议明确提出，推出⼀批务实管⽤的增量政策，优化财政⽀出安排，
⽤好地⽅政府债务结存限额，综合运⽤并适时调整货币政策⼯具，研究出台稳定房地产市场、
促进就业增收等政策措施。上海易居房地产研究院副院⻓严跃进解读称，这意味着四季度尤其
是 10 ⽉份或将迎来系列政策出台。
逻辑链⼆：上海现房销售新政落地，京沪⽰范效应显现。

商品住房销售制度的通知〉的实施意⻅》，提出加强预售管理、实施现房销售、推⾏主办银⾏制
度、加⼤融资⽀持⼒度等 6 ⽅⾯措施。中指研究院上海数据总经理张⽂静指出，上海采⽤新⽼划
断、分类施策的总体框架，与北京⼀脉相承，京沪两地细则相继落地，为其他城市提供了可复
制的范本，预计更多城市将加快跟进出台执⾏细则。

幅 10% ⾄ 20% ；下游 PCB ⼚商已陆续启动重新报价，产业链价格传导机制由此彻底打通。在 AI

算⼒基建的驱动下，⾼阶 HDI 、 IC 载板等⾼端赛道需求持续⾼增， BT 载板材料交期已拉⻓⾄ 16

业化路径的⾸次系统定调，市场将其解读为固态电池从 " 技术可⾏ " ⾛向 " 经济可⾏ " 的政策起点。
逻辑链⼆：免征消费税叠加排产回暖，盈利弹性打开。
财政部、海关总署、税务总局此前联合发布公告，明确⾃ 2026 年 9 ⽉ 1 ⽇起⾄ 2028 年 12 ⽉ 31 ⽇，
对钠离⼦电池、固态电池、燃料电池及部分光伏电池免征消费税，政策红利直接增厚企业盈利
空间。中信建投研报显⽰， 8 ⽉中国锂电全市场总排产约 304GWh 、环⽐增⻓ 7.4% 超出预期，储
能电芯排产 125GWh 、环⽐净增约 10GWh ，海外⼤储项⽬集中备货推动储能跃升为第⼀增⻓引
擎。
中 信 证 券 表 ⽰ ， 固 态 电 池产 业 化 发 展 持 续 加 速 ， 预 计 2 0 3 0 年 全 球 固 态 电 池 总 出 货 将 超

700GWh ，其中全固态电池超 200GWh ；中国半固态电池已经量产，实现对欧美⽇韩的弯道超
⻋，全固态电池加速追赶，整体⽬标是 2026—2027 年实现上⻋和⼩批量产、 2030 年实现商业
化，成⻓曲线陡峭。
中银证券认为， 2026 年全球新能源汽⻋销量有望保持较快增⻓，带动电池和材料需求增⻓，新
技术⽅⾯固态电池和钠离⼦电池产业化逐步落地，建议关注相关材料和设备企业的验证进展。
创业板⼜现⼤⾁签
创业板新股鸿富诚今⽇上市，发⾏价为 76.86 元 / 股，该股股价盘中最⾼⼀度冲到 630 元以上，⼤
涨逾 700% ，中⼀签浮盈超 27 万。
消息⾯上，鸿富诚的前⼗⼤股东中，有华为旗下哈勃科技。阿⾥云、通富微电、新易盛等⾏业
⻰头及深圳国资背景创投机构亦参与战略配售。
鸿富诚是国家级专精特新重点 “ ⼩巨⼈ ” 企业，专注热管理、电磁屏蔽和吸波等先进电⼦功能材料
的研发与产业化，产品主要包括导热界⾯材料、电磁屏蔽及吸波材料，是保障数据中⼼（ AI ⾼功
率芯⽚、光模块）、智能汽⻋、 5G 通信及消费电⼦等领域可靠运⾏的关键材料。

业绩⽅⾯， 2023 年、 2024 年、 2025 年，鸿富诚的营业收⼊分别为 2.60 亿元、 3.30 亿元、 7.07 亿
元，归⺟净利润分别为 0.35 亿元、 0.71 亿元、 2.69 亿元。 2026 年上半年，公司营业收⼊为 4.35
亿元，同⽐增⻓ 66.29% ；归⺟净利润为 1.76 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 94.60% 。

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the importance of supply
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
09:47 September 25 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s only data release this week was the August Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised to
the upside, rising 39.5k. But, once again, new labour demand was more than offset by supply as the
participation rate bounced 0.2ppts higher to 67.1%, just shy of January 2025’s 115-year high of 67.2%.

As a result, the unemployment rate increased from 4.5% to 4.6% and was close to rounding up to 4.7%
(4.65% to two decimal places).

### Page 2

There may be some residual seasonality in August’s print related to the new survey collection model.
However, the underlying trend is still consistent with a gradual easing in the labour market, seeing the
unemployment rate drift higher. We expect this to continue through to the first half of 2027.
The strength in labour force participation also speaks to the broader issue of Australia’s supply
capacity. As discussed by Chief Economist Luci Ellis, the RBA’s assumptions around participation,

productivity and the NAIRU collectively skew towards a weak assessment of supply capacity. For
participation in particular, the RBA’s near-term assumptions are downbeat compared to recent
demographic trends and also sit below Treasury’s longer-term projections as set out in the 2026 IGR.
As above, the August LFS emphasises that slack could emerge more quickly than the RBA’s forecasts
imply without an outright fall in employment.
Ahead of next week’s RBA meeting and inflation data, our latest state-level analysis provides

perspective on the persistence of underlying price pressures, particularly the importance of housing-
related components. For a broader assessment of the fiscal backdrop and the implications for the
states, our latest state fiscal update explores how the housing market correction and changes to NDIS
funding arrangements are pressuring state finances.
Before turning to this week’s events offshore, it is important to highlight that last Friday the Bank of

Japan raised its policy rate by 25bp to 1.25%. Policymakers maintained a constructive assessment of
the economy, pointing to resilient consumption, ongoing labour market tightness, and solid investment
spending. More importantly, the Bank expressed growing confidence that inflation is increasingly being
sustained by domestic wage and demand dynamics rather than imported cost shocks alone.
In our view, the emphasis placed on improving underlying inflation dynamics suggests policymakers

are becoming increasingly comfortable that inflation is able to be sustained by domestic demand and
wage growth. Normalisation is likely to remain gradual, but the Bank appears firmly on a path towards
further tightening, with rates likely to reach 1.5% by mid-2027.
This week, market participants have focused on discussions between the US and China on trade,
security and technology as President Xi visits Washington.
Preliminary discussions between US Treasury Secretary Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng

ahead of President Xi’s arrival in Washington were characterised by both sides as productive, and
Treasury Secretary Bessent subsequently announced that discussions in Washington would leave
trade negotiations for upcoming meetings between the two leaders in Shenzhen and Miami. To allow
room for this deferral, the current trade truce will be extended until January 2027. President Trump
subsequently outlined that discussions during President Xi’s Washington visit would instead focus on

security, technology and AI.
A more constructive tone was also seen between the US and Iran overnight, if press reports are
accurate. According to Bloomberg and Reuters, sources suggest the US and Iran are again exploring a
staged deal which would see Iran re-open the Strait of Hormuz and the US lift its blockade of Iranian
ports. Negotiations are occurring on the sidelines of the UN General Assembly, with Qatari officials

### Page 3

Related articles
mediating.
President Trump continues to show little interest in a deal before November’s mid-term elections, but
the global economy is under increasing pressure as the price of Brent oil hovers between USD100 and
USD110 while refining spreads add additional impetus to refined product prices. The mooted
consideration of a potential US diesel export ban this week makes clear that the US is not immune
from this pressure despite having significant domestic energy supply and refining capacity, relative to

their own demand.
The consequences of the current energy supply shock and other disruptions the US have experienced
over the past year remain front of mind for FOMC members. Those speaking this week such as NY and
Cleveland Fed Presidents Williams and Hammack highlighted the risk to inflation expectations from a
series of supply shocks when the economy is fully employed, and the likelihood of an additional hike(s)
being required to rein in current inflation momentum and risks. Williams noted that expectations of

another hike by year end are "reasonable" and that the FOMC have "a lot of work to do". Debt market’s
concern remains acute, having now almost priced in four hikes over the coming year and taken the US
10-year above 5.00%, currently 5.20%.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh được 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng 
hơn; trong khi triển vọng nhóm 
khách hàng công nghiệp bị ảnh 
hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 
785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025 
giúp sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế 
quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống 
của Việt Nam.

UBND TP.HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng 
mạnh từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh 
trong thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự 
án của doanh nghiệp ở mảng này sẽ vận hành hiệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và 
lợi nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với 
BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh 
 Biwase là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực cung cấp nước sạch với 
thị phần đạt 100% tại Bình Dương. 
Ngoài ra, công ty còn mở rộng sang 
lĩnh vực đầu tư nhà máy xử lý nước và 
chất thải rắn nhằm hoàn thiện hệ 
sinh thái dịch vụ hạ tầng môi trường 
tại khu vực. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Nắng nóng thúc đẩy tiêu thụ nước sạch khối dân cư, đem về tổng doanh thu cấp nước tại Bình Dương và 
Bình Phước năm 2026/2027 đạt 2,953/3,192 tỷ VND (6.8%/7.4%). 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Nhà máy nước Nhị Thành giai đoạn 3 đi vào hoạt động giúp tổng doanh thu nhà máy đạt lần lượt 213/247 tỷ 
VND (+16%/16% YoY) trong 2026/2027 và duy trì tăng trưởng trung bình 15% đến 2030. 
 Tham khảo trong báo cáo kỳ trước:

không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần

### Page 4

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh 
được thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng 
nóng hơn; trong khi triển vọng 
nhóm khách hàng công nghiệp bị 
ảnh hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan 
 Dự báo đồng thuận xác suất xảy ra El Nino ở mức xấp xỉ 100% từ 2H2026 kéo 
dài đến tháng đầu 2027 cho thấy cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt 
nắng nóng sẽ gay gắt và kéo dài hơn so với các năm trước. Đây là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY 
(+2.6/1.1 ppts). Trái lại, sau khi section 301 được công bố và có hiệu lực thay 
thế section 122, tình hình thuế quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho 
các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống của Việt Nam. Cụ thể, Section 301 không tự 
hết hạn và có nền tảng pháp lý chắc chắn (ít nhất là trong nhiệm kỳ của 
Trump); đồng thời áp dụng hạn ngạch thuế quan cho một lượng hàng dệt may 
nhất định của một số nước không có Việt Nam, khiến bối cảnh bất lợi cho các 
ngành sản xuất truyền thống. Tuy nhiên, với việc Bình Dương đang tập trung 
đầu tư hạ tầng khu công nghiệp hiện đại nhằm thu hút dòng vốn FDI vào các 
lĩnh vực công nghệ cao, sản lượng nước sạch sẽ được duy trì khi tiêu thụ khối 
sản xuất truyền thống được bù đắp bởi nhóm công nghệ cao. Theo đó, trong 
năm 2026, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng nhẹ dự báo sản lượng nước sạch tại Bình 
Dương & Bình Phước đạt 224.9 triệu m3 (+6.8% YoY), với mức tăng của nhóm 
sản xuất/sinh hoạt/hành chính lần lượt là 5.8%/7.5%/11% YoY.

Biểu đồ 4. Xác suất tăng dần của EL Nino

Nguồn: Bloomberg, KBSV tổng hợp

Mảng cấp nước: Tiến độ điều chỉnh 
giá bán nước gián đoạn do môi 
trường lạm phát leo cao 
 Do tác động của cuộc chiến Mỹ - Iran gây ra gián đoạn dòng chảy vận tải dầu 
tại eo biển Hormuz, giá nhiên liệu trong nước đã tăng mạnh từ đầu năm (Dầu 
Diesel - +64.3%, E5 RON 92 – +40%) và khiến cho tình hình lạm phát trong nước 
diễn biến tiêu cực: CPI bình quân 8 tháng năm 2026 tăng 4.5% với chỉ số giá 
nhóm nhà ở, điện nước, chất đốt và vật liệu xây dựng tăng cao nhất với 6.7% và 
giao thông tăng 5.4%. Trong bối cảnh đó, chúng tôi cho rằng tiến độ điều 
chỉnh giá bán nước tại các thị trường của BWE có khả năng bị đẩy lùi sang 
2027-2028 hoặc cho đến khi tình hình giá nhiên liệu ổn định trở lại. Trong dài 
hạn, khả năng đàm phán giá nước thành công là khá cao bởi: i) Thông tư 
145/2025/TT-BTC có hiệu lực từ 2026 là căn cứ để UBND quy định tỷ suất lợi 
nhuận định mức các dự án nhà máy nước; ii) BWE và cổ đông lớn là TDM dự 
kiến đầu tư và mở rộng công suất cung cấp – phân phối nước tại các vùng hẻo 
lánh nên có khả năng tăng áp lực đàm phán tăng giá nước tại Bình Dương. 
Trong kịch bản thận trọng, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng giá nước sạch trung bình khu

vực Bình Dương 1%/năm từ 2028 – 2030.

Đơn giá xử thu gom, vận chuyện và 
xử lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho 
phép cải thiện doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận của mảng này 
 UBND TP.HCM (bao gồm TP.HCM cũ, Bình Dương, Bà Rịa Vũng Tàu) quy định 
giá cụ thể đối với dịch vụ vận chuyển, xử lý chất thải rắn sinh hoạt trên địa bàn. 
Theo đó, quy định mới thay thế văn bản có hiệu lực từ 2016 sẽ chuyển hình 
thức tính giá đầy đủ theo 3 cấu phần: Thu gom, vận chuyển và Xử lý; với khung 
giá được xác định cho các phường đô thị và xã/nông thôn. So với quy định 
2016, quy định mới điều chỉnh tăng mạnh khung giá khoán đối với chủ nguồn 
thải nhỏ (Hộ gia đình tăng 80-184% đối với khu vực đô thị, 55-140% đối với khu 
vực nông thôn; Hộ kinh doanh nhỏ lẻ tăng 42-150% đối với khu vực đô thị, 20-
138% đối với khu vực nông thôn); đồng thời yêu cầu chủ thải tại nguồn và chủ 
nguồn thải lớn áp dụng khung giá thành (bao gồm giá thu gom, vận chuyển và 
xử lý) nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn chi phí liên quan tới các khâu xử 
lý rác thải thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng việc ban hành khung giá mới tính toán 
đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh phản ánh định hướng phát triển 
hạ tầng xử lý rác thải hiện đại của TP.HCM, đặt trọng tâm vào các nhà máy đốt 
rác phát điện thân thiện và an toàn với môi trường. Đối với doanh nghiệp hoạt 
động trong lĩnh vực môi trường nhiều kinh nghiệm như BWE, cơ chế thu gom, 
vận chuyển và xử lý minh bạch sẽ giúp thúc đẩy hiệu quả các dự án mà doanh 
nghiệp có kế hoạch triển khai trong tương lai. KBSV dự báo doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Nguồn: UBND TP .HCM, KBSV

Biwase thành công tăng vốn cổ 
phần lên 2,513 tỷ VND 
 Sau đợt chào báo cho cổ đông hiện hữu với giá chào bán 37,000 đồng/cổ 
phiếu, doanh nghiệp dự kiến huy động được khoảng 1,162.5 tỷ VND, nâng vốn 
điều lệ lên mức 2,513 tỷ VND. Khoản vốn bổ sung được dự kiến sử dụng cho ba 
mục đích chính: thanh toán nợ vay đến hạn (khoảng 773.3 tỷ đồng), đầu tư dự 
án nhà máy điện rác công suất 24MW (225 tỷ đồng) và mua cổ phần tại CTCP 
Cấp nước Ninh Thuận (hơn 164 tỷ đồng), qua đó biến doanh nghiệp này trở 
thành công ty liên kết. Dự kiến, lợi nhuận đóng góp từ công ty liên kết trong 
2026/2027 tiếp tục tăng trưởng tích cực, đạt 238.1/245.2 tỷ VND (40%/3% YoY).

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Về diễn biến thị trường, BWE hiện đang giao dịch quanh mức -1 độ lệch chuẩn 
trung bình 5 năm EV/EBITDA, phản ánh những bất ổn trong thương mại toàn 
cầu ảnh hưởng tới tiêu thụ nước phục vụ sản xuất. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi đánh 
giá triển vọng trong tương lai của mảng môi trường (bao gồm nước thải và rác 
thải) cộng với chiến lược đa dạng hóa địa bàn cung cấp nước sẽ bù đắp được 
KQKD tăng trưởng chậm lại.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA 
với BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ 
vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026. Mức EV/EBITDA mục tiêu cho 
việc định giá doanh nghiệp là 8.8 lần, tương ứng với mức trung bình 5 năm của 
doanh nghiệp, để phản ánh sự cải thiện KQKD đến từ mảng môi trường và cơ 
chế giảm giá mua nước từ TDM sẽ bù đắp sự giảm tốc của mảng cấp nước trong 
ngắn hạn. Với mức giá mục tiêu trên, cổ phiếu được định giá tương đương P/B 
1.7x cho 2026, phù hợp với việc ROE được kỳ vọng cải thiện lên mức 14.1%.

Hệ thống khuyến nghị

Hệ thống khuyến nghị đầu tư ngành 
(Dựa trên đánh giá triển vọng trong 6 tháng tới)  
Tích cực:

Tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 được dự 
báo khả quan so với cùng kỳ

NIM chịu áp lực trước mục tiêu tăng 
trưởng

Chất lượng tài sản suy giảm nhẹ do 
mặt bằng lãi suất ở mức cao

Khuyến nghị: MUA – Giá mục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 đạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đặc biệt là từ nhóm FDI trong bối cảnh dòng vốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp 
vốn cho các dự án hạ tầng và dự án BĐS ưu tiên, trong khi các mảng kinh doanh 
khác tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp lực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi suất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh của ngân hàng cũng như đánh giá các yếu tố rủi ro, 
chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 
25,950 VNĐ/CP - tiềm năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Cơ cấu tín dụng  Hoạt động kinh doanh

Ngân hàng TMCP Quân đội (MBB) được 
thành lập năm 1994 với định hướng hoạt 
động ban đầu là cung cấp các dịch vụ tài 
chính cho các doanh nghiệp quân đội. Sau 
hơn 30 năm phát triển, MBB hiện đã trở 
thành ngân hàng sở hữu quy mô tài sản và 
tín dụng lớn nhất trong nhóm NH TMCP tư 
nhân. Phát triển theo mô hình tập đoàn, 
MBB đi đầu trong lĩnh vực chuyển đổi số với 
số lượng khách hàng và tỷ lệ CASA đầu 
ngành. 
Nguồn: MBB, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Tăng trưởng tín dụng có thể không đạt kế hoạch 30% do rào cản từ bài toán huy động, nhưng vẫn ở 
mức vượt trội so với ngành. 
 Tham khảo trong báo cáo kỳ trước tại đây 
Dự báo NIM giảm so với 2025, nhưng lợi nhuận vẫn tăng khả quan nhờ tăng trưởng tín dụng.  Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

HOẠT ĐỘNG KINH DOANH 
Kết quả kinh doanh tích cực nhờ 
tăng trưởng thu nhập lãi thuần 
 Tín dụng trong 2Q2026 tăng mạnh so với quý trước, đạt 13.2% YTD cùng sự 
phục hồi của NIM đã giúp ngân hàng thiết lập kỷ lục mới về thu nhập lãi thuần 
với 16,894 tỷ đồng (+36.5% YoY). Thu nhập ngoài lãi tiếp tục thu hẹp trong quý 
này do hoạt động kinh doanh ngoại hối và chứng khoán kém tích cực, qua đó 
phần nào làm giảm tốc tăng trưởng tổng thu nhập hoạt động (+18.5% YoY). Chi 
phí dự phòng rủi ro đạt 4,247 tỷ đồng (-11.3% YoY), qua đó giúp LNTT tăng tích 
cực (+40.7% YoY), đạt 10,560 tỷ đồng. Lũy kế 1H2026, LNTT của ngân hàng đạt 
20,188 tỷ đồng, hoàn thành 51% kế hoạch BLĐ đề ra đầu năm.

hồi của NIM trong quý 2 đã hỗ trợ thu nhập lãi thuần tiếp tục

này khi thu từ các hoạt động kinh doanh chứng khoán và 
ngoại hối kém tích cực (lần lượt giảm -51.2% và -74.3% YoY).

từ hoạt động tư vấn và xử lý, định giá & quản lý tài sản chưa

liên ngân hàng (+15.2% YTD), trong khi kênh huy động truyền 
thống vẫn chưa khả quan (+4.6% YTD).

### Page 5

5

Tăng trưởng tín dụng 2026 được dự 
báo khả quan so với cùng kỳ  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự báo tăng trưởng tín dụng cả năm 2026 ở mức 27%, vượt 
trội hơn mặt bằng chung của ngành nhờ lợi thế tham gia nhận CGBB. Cụ thể: 
− Xét về phân khúc khách hàng, động lực chính kỳ vọng sẽ đến từ phân 
khúc KHDN, trong đó nổi bật là nhóm FDI khi ngân hàng đã chủ động 
thành lập khối Khách hàng FDI chuyên biệt trong bối cảnh dòng vốn 
FDI vào Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực.  
− Xét về lĩnh vực, chúng tôi cho rằng cho vay kinh doanh BĐS sẽ tăng 
trưởng vượt trội với kỳ vọng ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp các dự án 
hạ tầng và dự án BĐS ưu tiên (BĐS KCN, NOXH). Ngân hàng cho biết tỷ 
trọng dư nợ mảng này dự kiến sẽ được duy trì trong biên độ mục tiêu 
13±2% tổng dư nợ tín dụng. Chúng tôi nghiêng về khả năng ngân hàng 
sẽ bám sát cận trên của mục tiêu (15%), nhằm tận dụng dư địa tăng 
trưởng từ các dự án ưu tiên, đồng thời duy trì kiểm soát chất lượng tài 
sản an toàn. Trong khi đó, dư nợ cho vay các mảng khác dự kiến sẽ ở 
mức thấp hơn tăng trưởng chung do môi trường vĩ mô còn nhiều khó 
khăn.

Biểu đồ 8. Cơ cấu tín dụng 
Tỷ trọng cho vay kinh doanh BĐS liên tục gia tăng 
trong các quý gần đây, trong khi cơ cấu cho vay 
thương mại và chế biến chế tạo có xu hướng giảm. 
Tính tới hết 1H2026, tăng trưởng cho vay kinh doanh 
BĐS, thương mại, và chế biến chế tạo lần lượt đạt 
38.2%, 4.9%, và 9.3% YTD.

Nguồn: MBB, KBSV

NIM chịu áp lực giảm so với 2025  Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM cả năm sẽ giảm so với 2025 (-15bps YoY) dựa 
trên luận điểm:  
(1) Ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục dịch chuyển cơ cấu huy động sang các kênh có 
chi phí vốn (COF) cao hơn nhằm đáp ứng mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín 
dụng cao, trong bối cảnh cạnh tranh huy động trên thị trường 1 vẫn 
diễn ra gay gắt. Bên cạnh đó, ngân hàng cho biết dự kiến sẽ phát hành 
10-15 nghìn tỷ trái phiếu tier 2 trong 2H2026 với COF tương đối cao. 
(2) Với mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng cao, chúng tôi cho rằng ngân hàng 
sẽ duy trì lãi suất cho vay ở mức cạnh tranh so với mặt bằng chung của 
ngành. Các khoản vay dự kiến vẫn sẽ tiếp tục tái định giá theo mặt 
bằng lãi suất hiện tại, tuy nhiên tốc độ sẽ chậm lại so với quý 2, do đó 
lợi suất IEA dự kiến sẽ tăng chậm hơn COF trong 2H2026.

Chất lượng tài sản suy giảm trong 
tầm kiểm soát  
 Trong 2Q2026, tỷ lệ hình thành nợ xấu ở mức 1.2% (-16bps QoQ), tương ứng 
4,142 tỷ đồng nợ xấu phát sinh mới, và tỷ lệ nợ xấu tăng nhẹ 3bps so với quý 
trước. Nợ xấu tăng trong quý này chủ yếu tới từ phân khúc bán lẻ (do hoạt động 
kinh doanh và giá đất của khách hàng tại địa phương bị ảnh hưởng sau khi sáp

Cho cả năm 2026, chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps

Định giá – Khuyến nghị: MUA – Giá 
mục tiêu 25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá là P/B và Chiết khấu lợi nhuận thặng 
dư để tìm ra mức giá hợp lý cho cổ phiếu MBB. 
(1) Phương pháp định giá P/B 
Chúng tôi điều chỉnh P/B mục tiêu xuống mức 1.5x trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất neo cao và thanh khoản ngân hàng còn nhiều khó khăn. Dù vậy, 
P/B mục tiêu vẫn cao hơn so với P/B trung bình 5 năm của ngân 
hàng nhằm phản ánh kỳ vọng tích cực đối với việc room ngoại của 
ngân hàng được nới lên 49% theo Nghị định 69/2025/NĐ-CP. 
(2) Phương pháp chiết khấu lợi nhuận thặng dư  
Bên cạnh đó, chúng tôi kết hợp sử dụng thêm phương pháp chiết 
khấu lợi nhuận thặng dư để phản ánh rủi ro hệ thống và kỳ vọng dài 
hạn. 
Khuyến nghị MUA  với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 25,950 VNĐ/CP - tiềm 
năng tăng giá 26.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 17/09/2026.

Document 374: United States _ A shock-resistant expansion (2026-09-24)

Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
A SHOCK-RESISTANT EXPANSION
US growth remains resilient despite successive shocks. According to our forecasts, it is expected to reach 2.2% in 2026 and
2.3% in 2027. Its main driver is still investment in artificial intelligence (AI), which has remained entirely unaffected by the
energy crisis. The labour market has seen a marked improvement in job creation and a fall in the unemployment rate. Core

inflation remains stubbornly high and above the Federal Reserve (Fed)’s target. Against this backdrop, the Fed has begun a
hiking cycle in September, which, according to our scenario, would bring the Fed Funds target to 4.50% (upper limit) by Q1
2027. At the same time, long-term rates are under upward pressure.
1  This includes: energy and communications; data centres (facilities); IT equipment and peripherals; communication equipment (equipment); software; and research and develop-

ment (intellectual-property products).
THE ENERGY SHOCK IS NOT HOLDING BACK GROWTH
Driven by AI-related investment, US growth is holding up despite ri -
sing energy inflation. We forecast GDP growth at 2.2% in 2026 and
2.3% in 2027 on an annual average basis. The AI-related investment
cycle1 (+14.8% y/y in H1 2026) is expected to continue, accompanied by
potential productivity gains. However, other private-investment seg -
ments (including residential property) remain sluggish. In addition, the

rising cost of capital could accentuate its ‘K-shaped’ profile. Household
consumption is expected, on the whole, to benefit from the rebound in
job creation and to remain above 2% y/y through to the end of 2027.
In H1 2026, it held up against resurging inflation due to an average
year-on-year increase of 11.5% in tax refunds and a savings rate stan-
ding at its lowest level since 2022 (2.8% in Q2).
RECORD CAPITAL-RAISING DRIVEN BY AI
This year, AI-related investments are driving massive capital-raising

by companies through loans, bond issues or share placements. This
trend is set to continue given the seemingly colossal scale of the fun -
ding requirements, even as risk premia in the bond market begin to
widen. According to their statements, the five tech giants could invest
nearly USD 900 bn this year and over USD 1,000 bn next year—more
than double the figure for 2025. Spurred on by regulatory easing, the
major US banks have announced that they will mobilise significant re-

sources to support projects considered strategic for the United States
(AI, defence, energy and infrastructure). While funding for the AI boom
appears unaffected by rising long-term interest rates, these latter are
likely to continue to weigh on household demand for mortgages. Since
the start of the conflict in the Middle East, 30-year mortgage rates
have risen by 78 bp (to 6.76% at the start of September). Conversely,
consumer lending remained buoyant in H1 2026 and is supporting

household spending.
TWO-SPEED GROWTH IN INDUSTRY
Industrial production rebounded slightly in 2025 (+1.1%), driven by AI
and aerospace. We forecast an acceleration to +1.5% in 2026 and +2%
in 2027. Investment in AI is bolstering the electronics sector, while the
(civil and military) aerospace sector is rebounding as order backlogs
are cleared and defence requirements rise. Conversely, sectors ex -
posed to rising tariffs (in particular, the steel, aluminium and auto -

motive sectors) are still on the back burner, contrary to the objective
of these measures. This positive momentum in industrial production,
while concentrated, is expected to continue, bolstered by the recove -
ry in business sentiment. The ISM Manufacturing Index has been in
expansionary territory since early 2026, despite ongoing pressures on
prices and supply chains.

LABOUR MARKET: A LOW UNEMPLOYMENT RATE ON A DOWNWARD
TREND
Job creation (non-farm payrolls) has rebounded, averaging +80,000 per
month since the start of the year, compared with +10,000 in 2025. This
growth is no longer limited to the healthcare sector. Excluding health-
care, job creation is up by an average of +38,000 in 2026, compared

with -47,000 in 2025. Manufacturing and construction, two sectors
driven by AI, are contributing to this rebound. At the same time, the
labour force has not grown since January 2025 due to the decline in the
foreign-born workforce (down 4.2% cumulatively over the period). Ex -
cluding the impact of COVID-19, the labour force participation rate is at
its lowest level since the 1970s, standing at around 61.5%. The rebound
in labour demand and persistent pressure on the labour supply have

brought the unemployment rate down to 4.1% in August 2026 (a 0.3 pp
fall since the start of the year). The jobless rate is expected to fall to
3.9% at the end of 2027 (4.2% corresponds to full employment, accor -
ding to the CBO) thanks to a job creation rate of around 40,000–60,000
per month.
INFLATION: STRUCTURALLY ABOVE TARGET
The rise in oil prices has triggered a rebound in CPI inflation. The
contribution from the energy component (+1.2 pp) largely accounts for

the rise in inflation since February. A slowdown was seen (from +4.2%
to +3.4% y/y) between the May peak and August. This is expected to
continue due to base effects on energy prices. We anticipate the trough
to be +1.8% y/y in Q2 2027 before a stabilisation at around +2.3% y/y
later in the year.
Meanwhile, core inflation is expected to remain above the Fed’s 2%
target, and above +2.5% y/y in 2027. This highlights the structural
nature of inflation, beyond short-term fluctuations linked to energy.

Non-housing services are expected to show significant inertia, with in-
flation hovering at around +3% y/y by the end of 2027. The potential for
disinflation also appears limited for housing-related services, as the
rise in rents shows no signs of abating. Finally, inflation on non-energy
goods is slowing as the effects of tariffs fade. However, the AI cycle is
exerting upward pressure through the general rise in the cost of elec -
tronic goods.
FED: GREEN LIGHT FOR RATE HIKES

The FOMC has embarked on a cycle of rate increases. The Fed Funds
target was raised to 4.0% (+25 bp) at the September meeting, and we
anticipate two further rises that would bring the policy rate to 4.5% in
January 2027. These three rises (made possible by reassuring develop-
ments in employment and economic activity) are intended to reverse
the three cuts in 2025 adopted to counter the ‘downside risks’ to the
labour market at that time. As a result, the committee can recalibrate

its policy towards ‘price stability’ and, through its interest rate deci -
sions, reflect the priority given to this objective in its communications.
The risks point towards further monetary tightening: in our baseline
scenario, core inflation would remain sticky and the fall in unemploy -
ment would give the FOMC room for manoeuvre to raise rates further.
PUBLIC FINANCES: UNDERLYING DETERIORATION AND PRESSURE ON
LONG- TERM RATES
According to our estimates, the federal deficit is set to widen by 0.7 pp

to -6.6% of GDP in 2026 (by 0.4 pp to -3.1% for the primary deficit),
weighed down by falling tariff revenue (refunds of tariffs already col -
lected and lower-than-expected receipts). In 2027, the end of this one-
off effect is expected to lead to a marginal improvement to -6.3% (to
-2.8% for the primary deficit). The growing burden of interest payments
(3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 3.6% in 2027, following 3.2% in 2025) and
the conflict in Iran (affecting the military budget) are weighing on the

budget balance and federal-debt dynamics. Federal debt is still on an
upward trend, with no particular change in direction, exceeding 100% of
GDP (101.9%, up 3.3 pp, in 2026, and 103.5% in 2027). It is expected to
reach 110% by the end of the decade against a backdrop of budget de-
ficits of close to 6% of GDP , assuming fiscal policy remains unchanged.
Furthermore, the mid-term elections (which see the entire House of
Representatives and one-third of the Senate up for re-election) could

result in a divided Congress or in both chambers changing hands to
the Democratic Party. Should the former occur, this would increase the
risk of a legislative deadlock, while the presidential veto would limit
the Democrats’ ability to influence fiscal policy in the latter scenario.
Against this backdrop, pressure on the bond market is likely to continue
in the coming months. Treasury issuance is rising, fuelled by increa -
sing spending requirements (in areas such as AI, defence and supply

chains). At the same time, shifts in investor demographics are increa-
sing demand volatility, and issuances by companies in the artificial
intelligence sector are intensifying competition for capital. We antici -
pate a 10-year yield of 4.95% in Q1 2027, before a slight easing brought
about by Fed rate rises, which would lend credibility to the price-sta -
bility objective. However, according to our forecast, 10-year yield is
expected to remain close to a 20-year high, at 4.80% in Q4 2027.

THE US RELIES ON IMPORTS FOR ITS GROWTH
The US trade deficit has recently started to rise again. According to
our forecasts, it is expected to account for the same proportion of GDP
in 2026 as it did in 2024 (4.4%). This trend is driven by imports of
AI-related capital goods, which are set to rise from 1.2% to 2.1% of GDP
between 2022 and 2026. Investment in AI is, in fact, import-intensive
(Chart 2), which dampens its positive impact on growth and highlights

the US’s dependence on foreign supplies in a cycle that it is neverthe-
less spearheading.
The additional tariffs have failed to reverse the trend in the trade de -
ficit. Furthermore, the IEEPA tariffs struck down by the Supreme Court
were replaced, in July 2026, by ‘Section 301’ tariffs (forced labour) ran-
ging from 10% to 12.5%. These tariffs target 60 countries and have
been introduced on top of existing sector-specific tariffs (steel, alumi -

nium and automotive). Following these adjustments, the statutory rate
(measured against the 2024 import structure) remained slightly above
10%. The main risks relate to potential new sector-specific tariffs and
the renegotiation of the USMCA.
Article completed on 17 September 2026

美
